|beeneliminated or reduced to the point that involuntary services are no<br>longer required.<br>If the child(ren) has already been adjudicated abused, neglected, and/or<br>dependent and, at a later date, the agency decides that nonsecure custody is<br>necessary to protect the child(ren), the agency must file a nonsecure custody<br>order to remove the child(ren) that contains language stating that the removal<br>is in the child(ren)'s best interest or that the child(ren) remaining in the home<br>is contrary to the welfare of the child(ren).<br>In cases where the juvenile has been adjudicated and has not been removed<br>from the parent, guardian, or custodian the court must conduct a review<br>hearing within 90 days of the initial dispositional hearing and then every six<br>months thereafter.<br>The Clerk of Court must make notice at least 15 days prior to the review<br>hearing to:<br>•<br>The parent, guardian, or custodian;<br>•<br>Thechild or youth regardless of age;<br>•<br>The local county child welfare agency involved with the family;<br>•<br>The Guardian Ad Litem; and<br>•<br>Any other person the courtdeems necessary.<br>The court will review the appropriateness of thechild's continued placement<br>at each hearing. |Documentation of CPS In-Home Services mustinclude a description of: <br>•<br>Home visits, school visits, and any other family member contact;<br>•<br>Contacts withSafety Network and support members as well asextended family, services providers, etc.;<br>•<br>Meetings and decisions made;<br>•<br>Observations regarding family interaction and relationships, engagement in services andbehavioral changes<br>•<br>Services,activities, andinterventionsagreed upon andprovided, arranged for or coordinated;<br>•<br>All diligent efforts to make appropriate contacts, if not achieved;<br>•<br>The support needed requiringcontinuing agency involvement;<br>•<br>The family's progress or barriers toward case goals (through use ofIHFCP); <br>•<br>Case conferences with the group/unit, including any two-level decisions made by the supervisor and caseworker;<br>•<br>Justification for any missed policy or protocol requirements (missed timeframes, etc.);<br>•<br>Any discussions regardingongoing safety and/or risk of maltreatment;<br>•<br>Any new allegations and actions taken;<br>•<br>All discussions regarding thewell-being needs of the child(ren); and<br>•<br>Any other efforts by the local county child welfare agency to achieve safety and protection, family preservation, and<br>prevention of futuremaltreatment.<br>Documentation must be maintained in the'Case Contact' section of PATH NC. It mustbe current within seven days of any case activity<br>or action.<br>The following information must be included for each documentation entry regarding a contact or attempted contact:<br>•<br>Date of each contact and name of each person contacted;<br>•<br>Purpose of the contact;<br>•<br>Significant family/child/parent issues;<br>•<br>Type of contact (phone, face-to-face, home visit, etc.) and location for all face-to-face contacts;<br>•<br>Individual interview with each child present;<br>•<br>Observations regarding each person and the environment for face-to-face contacts; and/or<br>•<br>Diligent efforts to make a contact and date of the efforts, what were efforts to make this contact (phone call, home visit<br>but no one home, etc.).|For additional<br>guidance see<br>"Documentation"<br>Cross Function<br>Topics in theNC<br>Child Welfare<br>manual.<br>PATH NC allows<br>you to capture all<br>of this information<br>and auto-populates<br>information that<br>has already been<br>previously entered<br>in the system such<br>as demographic<br>information and<br>the IHFCP.|For additional<br>guidance see<br>"Documentation"<br>Cross Function<br>Topics in theNC<br>Child Welfare<br>manual.<br>PATH NC allows<br>you to capture all<br>of this information<br>and auto-populates<br>information that<br>has already been<br>previously entered<br>in the system such<br>as demographic<br>information and<br>the IHFCP.|For additional<br>guidance see<br>"Documentation"<br>Cross Function<br>Topics in theNC<br>Child Welfare<br>manual.<br>PATH NC allows<br>you to capture all<br>of this information<br>and auto-populates<br>information that<br>has already been<br>previously entered<br>in the system such<br>as demographic<br>information and<br>the IHFCP.|

# New Reports or Allegations Received During In Home Services

| | Policy | | | Legal Basis | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|Any new allegation and/or incident that meets the statutory definition of<br>abuse, neglect, or dependency received at any time during CPS In-Home<br>Services must be documented inPATH-NC as a new report and screened<br>through Intake on the Structured Decision Making Screening and<br>Response Tool, andif it meets the threshold a CPS-A must be conducted.|Any new allegation and/or incident that meets the statutory definition of<br>abuse, neglect, or dependency received at any time during CPS In-Home<br>Services must be documented inPATH-NC as a new report and screened<br>through Intake on the Structured Decision Making Screening and<br>Response Tool, andif it meets the threshold a CPS-A must be conducted.|Any new allegation and/or incident that meets the statutory definition of<br>abuse, neglect, or dependency received at any time during CPS In-Home<br>Services must be documented inPATH-NC as a new report and screened<br>through Intake on the Structured Decision Making Screening and<br>Response Tool, andif it meets the threshold a CPS-A must be conducted.|N.C.G.S.§7B-302<br>Section 106 (b)(2)(A)(xviii) of the Child Abuse Prevention and Treatment<br>Act (CAPTA)|N.C.G.S.§7B-302<br>Section 106 (b)(2)(A)(xviii) of the Child Abuse Prevention and Treatment<br>Act (CAPTA)|N.C.G.S.§7B-302<br>Section 106 (b)(2)(A)(xviii) of the Child Abuse Prevention and Treatment<br>Act (CAPTA)|
||Protocol - Whatyou must do|Protocol - Whatyou must do|Protocol - Whatyou must do|Protocol - Whatyou must do||
|See Assessments Manual (PATH NC) for policy, protocol, and guidance for completing the CPS-A.<br>The report must be assessed independently of the In-Home Services case.<br>If a new danger indicator arises during an In-Home case (for example, the case was opened for severe neglect and a new allegation of sexual abuse is<br>made), the In-Home caseworker must make a report to Intake. If Intake accepts the report, a new SDM Safety Assessment must be completed that<br>addresses all current and newly identified danger indicators. Any new allegation or incident that meets the statutory definition of abuse, neglect, or<br>dependency during CPS In-Home Services must be entered in PATH-NC as a new report and screened through Intake using the SDM Screening and<br>Response Tool. If the report meets the screening threshold, a CPS Assessment must be initiated. See Assessments Manual (PATH NC) for policy,<br>protocol, and guidance for completing the CPS-A.<br>If the report is not accepted, the In-Home caseworker is still responsible for addressing the concern. In cases where there is not a new danger<br>indicator identified, but the circumstances require a revised Safety Plan, a new Progress to Case Closure Guide (PCCG) and revised Safety Plan must be<br>completed.<br>CASE DECISIONS FOR INVESTIGATIVE ASSESSMENTS<br>If it is determined that the new allegations are true:<br>`o` The finding must be to Substantiate;<br>`o` TheIn-Home caseworker must notify the family of the decision; and<br>`o` Any newly identified safety and risk factors must be addressed in the service delivery of the existing IHFCP. <!-- page: 57 -->
## New Reports or Allegations Received During In Home Services

o The In-Home caseworker must notify the family of the decision not to substantiate the new allegations but explain that the ongoing

CPS In-Home Services would continue based on the original allegations until successful completion of the IHFCP.

CPS ASSESSMENT CASE DECISIONS FOR FAMILY ASSESSMENTS If it is determined that the new allegations require CPS In-Home Services:

o The agency's case decision must be Child Protective Services Needed; o The In-Home caseworker must notify the family of the decision; and o Any newly identified safety and risk factors must be addressed in the service delivery of the existing IHFCP. If the only needs that are identified are those that were uncovered during the previous assessment and there is no new maltreatment identified:

o The case decision must be Child Protective Services Not Recommended; o There must be concise documentation in the record stating that the safety and risk factors related to the original assessment continue

and services must continue to reduce the original safety and risk of harm through the provision of CPS In-Home Services; and o The In-Home caseworker must notify the family of the decision of Child Protective Services Not Recommended regarding the new

allegations but explain that the ongoing CPS In-Home Services would continue based on the original allegations until successful completion of the IHFCP.

BIRTH OF CHILD ON OPEN IN-HOME SERVICES CASE

Whenever a child is born in a family open for In-Home Services, a new report must be generated in PATH-NC and documented in the Structured Decision-Making Screening and Response Tool in Intake for screening.

IN-HOME SERVICES

# Transfer of In Home Services to Another County

| | Policy | | | | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|When the family relocates to another county in the state, the agency must request the new county of residence accept the In-Home Services case and<br>provide CPS In-Home Services to the family.|When the family relocates to another county in the state, the agency must request the new county of residence accept the In-Home Services case and<br>provide CPS In-Home Services to the family.|When the family relocates to another county in the state, the agency must request the new county of residence accept the In-Home Services case and<br>provide CPS In-Home Services to the family.|When the family relocates to another county in the state, the agency must request the new county of residence accept the In-Home Services case and<br>provide CPS In-Home Services to the family.|When the family relocates to another county in the state, the agency must request the new county of residence accept the In-Home Services case and<br>provide CPS In-Home Services to the family.|When the family relocates to another county in the state, the agency must request the new county of residence accept the In-Home Services case and<br>provide CPS In-Home Services to the family.|
||Protocol - Whatyou must do|||Guidance - Howyou should do it||
|When a county learns that a family has relocated, and the county has the new address for the<br>family:<br>•<br>The original county must notify the new county within 24 hours for high risk or 48 hours<br>for moderate risk cases;<br>•<br>The new county of residence must confirm the family's address within 72 hours;<br>•<br>The original county must share its entire case record with the new county within seven<br>business days; and<br>~~•~~<br>The new county of residence is responsible for the provision of CPS In-Home Services as<br>soon as the agency is aware that the family has moved into the county, including<br>responsibility for overall case management and updating theIHFCP. <br>EXCEPTION DUE TO COURT INVOLVEMENT<br>When there is juvenile court involvement:<br>•<br>CPS In-Home Services should not transfer to another county;<br>•<br>If the original county determines that transfer is appropriate due to the circumstances<br>of the case, then prior to the transfer of custody or a change of venue, a hearing must<br>be held giving each involved county the right to appear and plead their position;<br>•<br>Should a county elect not to be present at the change of venue hearing, all rights to<br>argue against the transfer are forfeited;<br>•<br>The county with venue must notify all involved counties of any changes in venue.|When a county learns that a family has relocated, and the county has the new address for the<br>family:<br>•<br>The original county must notify the new county within 24 hours for high risk or 48 hours<br>for moderate risk cases;<br>•<br>The new county of residence must confirm the family's address within 72 hours;<br>•<br>The original county must share its entire case record with the new county within seven<br>business days; and<br>~~•~~<br>The new county of residence is responsible for the provision of CPS In-Home Services as<br>soon as the agency is aware that the family has moved into the county, including<br>responsibility for overall case management and updating theIHFCP. <br>EXCEPTION DUE TO COURT INVOLVEMENT<br>When there is juvenile court involvement:<br>•<br>CPS In-Home Services should not transfer to another county;<br>•<br>If the original county determines that transfer is appropriate due to the circumstances<br>of the case, then prior to the transfer of custody or a change of venue, a hearing must<br>be held giving each involved county the right to appear and plead their position;<br>•<br>Should a county elect not to be present at the change of venue hearing, all rights to<br>argue against the transfer are forfeited;<br>•<br>The county with venue must notify all involved counties of any changes in venue.|When a county learns that a family has relocated, and the county has the new address for the<br>family:<br>•<br>The original county must notify the new county within 24 hours for high risk or 48 hours<br>for moderate risk cases;<br>•<br>The new county of residence must confirm the family's address within 72 hours;<br>•<br>The original county must share its entire case record with the new county within seven<br>business days; and<br>~~•~~<br>The new county of residence is responsible for the provision of CPS In-Home Services as<br>soon as the agency is aware that the family has moved into the county, including<br>responsibility for overall case management and updating theIHFCP. <br>EXCEPTION DUE TO COURT INVOLVEMENT<br>When there is juvenile court involvement:<br>•<br>CPS In-Home Services should not transfer to another county;<br>•<br>If the original county determines that transfer is appropriate due to the circumstances<br>of the case, then prior to the transfer of custody or a change of venue, a hearing must<br>be held giving each involved county the right to appear and plead their position;<br>•<br>Should a county elect not to be present at the change of venue hearing, all rights to<br>argue against the transfer are forfeited;<br>•<br>The county with venue must notify all involved counties of any changes in venue.|The childis required to be seen within 72 hours of<br>the notification to the new county that the family<br>has relocated to their area. Both counties should<br>discuss whether immediate contact should be<br>made with the family to assure the safety of the<br>childbased on the most recent circumstances.<br>Please see Administrative Policy for additional<br>information.|The childis required to be seen within 72 hours of<br>the notification to the new county that the family<br>has relocated to their area. Both counties should<br>discuss whether immediate contact should be<br>made with the family to assure the safety of the<br>childbased on the most recent circumstances.<br>Please see Administrative Policy for additional<br>information.|The childis required to be seen within 72 hours of<br>the notification to the new county that the family<br>has relocated to their area. Both counties should<br>discuss whether immediate contact should be<br>made with the family to assure the safety of the<br>childbased on the most recent circumstances.<br>Please see Administrative Policy for additional<br>information.|
